3 AUDIO PTLARSAETIONS OF TRANSACTIONS

Audit of transactions of the Government departments, their field formations as
well as that of the autonomous bodies brought out several instances of lapses
in management of resources and failures in the observance of the norms of
propriety and economy. These have been presented in the succeeding
paragraphs under broad objective heads.

3.

Fraud and detection of fraud

Audit of financial transactions, test checked in some departments of the
Government and their field functionaries showed instance of fraudulent
payments. The cases of fraudulent payments are discussed in the following
paragraphs:

HOME DEPARTMENT (POLICE)

3.1.1 Fraudulent drawals from the treasury

Rupees 71 lakh was fraudulently drawn from the Treasury at Rae
Bareli as the prescribed system was not adhered to in the office of the
Commandant, 2507 Battalion, Provincial Armed Constabulary, Rae
Bareli.

As per General Provident Fund (GPF) Rules, 1985, GPF accounts of the
employees of Group C category is maintained by the Office of AG (A&E),
Allahabad. The maintenance of GPF accounts of employees of Group D
category is the responsibility of the Heads of Offices. In case, a Group D
category employee is promoted to the Group C category, the balances of his
GPF accounts should be transferred to the office of AG (A&E), Allahabad for
maintenance. Besides, as per provisions contained in the Budget Manual', the
Drawing and Disbursing Officer (DDO) must reconcile each drawl by
obtaining DDO Reconciliation Statement from the treasury and endorse the
variations/ additional drawls to the Treasury Officer in the verification note.

The head constables and constables of Provincial Armed Constabulary (PAC)
are employees of Group C and D categories respectively.

Scrutiny of the records of the office of the Commandant, 257 Battalion, PAC,
Rae Bareli revealed (June 2012) that there were 116 head constables in the
Battalion as of May 2013, who were promoted from the post of constables.
However, the outstanding balances of GPF of 82 out of 116 head constables
were not transferred on their promotion to the post of Group C category by the
Commandant to the office of AG (A&E), Allahabad for maintenance of their
GPF accounts.

1 The drawing and disbursing officer must obtain reconciliation statement in Form B.M. 5 (in triplicate) duly signed
by the Treasury Officer by the 5" of the ensuing month and reconcile every drawal (voucher) from the treasury. The
reconciliation statement must contain voucher number, date, grant number and all level of heads of accounts
including the standard object of expenditure.

ee)


dit Report (General y ended 31 March 2013

Further, it was also observed that the Commandant sanctioned permanent
advances of हैं 1.07 crore to the constables during September 2011 to April
2012’ for withdrawals from GPF accounts maintained by him. The head
constable of the Battalion, holding the charge of Treasury Hawaldar, prepared
bills during the same period for withdrawals of these advances from the
treasury at Rae Bareli and fraudulently added his own name (in 12 cases)/ the
name of his son (in one case)/ the names of other employees (in three cases)
and thus inflated the amount of the bill by हैं 71 lakh to % 1.78 crore. The
inflated bills bearing overwritings and cuttings over the amounts were sent to
the treasury at Rae Bareli during October 2011 to April 2012 for payments.
The Treasury Officer, Rae Bareli without taking cognizance of the overwriting
and cuttings over the amounts of the bills, in disregard of the provisions
contained in the Financial Handbook’, passed the bills for payments of
र₹ 1.78 crore and issued individual treasury cheques. These cheques were
deposited in the banks. The details of amounts sanctioned, amounts drawn
from the treasury etc. are given in Appendix-3.1.

Further, the Commandant received DDO Reconciliation Statement pertaining
to September 2011 to April 2012 from the treasury for verification. However,
the Commandant sent the verification note to the Treasury Officer, at Rae
Bareli without reconciling the fraudulent drawls of = 71 lakh from the treasury
and as such these drawals remained undetected.

On this being pointed out in audit (27 June 2012), the Commandant, 25%
Battalion, PAC, Rae Bareli lodged (29 June 2012) a First Information Report
with the police against the head constable. The police, on investigation noticed
involvement of an Assistant Sub Inspector also and seized % 54.53 lakh,
besides a car and a bike from them. The police also filed (September 2012) a
case in the court where it was in progress as of July 2013. The departmental
enquiry was also in progress (as of July 2013). The seizures confirm that
% 71 lakh was withdrawn fraudulently from the treasury due to failure to
adhere to the prescribed system.

In reply, the Government confirmed (September 2013) the facts and figures.
During discussion (December 2013), the Government assured that
departmental action will be taken against the erring officials.

PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT

3.1.2 Fraudulent payment of salary

Failure to follow financial rules and the exercise of required internal
control/checks led to fraudulent payments of salary of = 10.36 lakh to two
beldars due to stayal in service beyond the date(s) of superannuation.

As per para 136 of Supplementary Rules (SR) of Financial Hand book Vol-II,
a service book shall be maintained for a Government Servant from the date of
his appointment by the Head of office who must ensure that all entries are

? Out of period of March 2009 10 May 2012 covered in audit,
3 In terms of Appendix XXIII [Para 428 (I)] of Financial Handbook Volume V Part II, the treasury officer is
responsible to ensure the bills/vouchers do not contain erasures and cuttings therein have been properly attested.

O—$_ — ui ___


Chapter 3 - Audit of Transactions

duly made and attested and that the service book contains no erasure or
overwriting. According to Government Order (October 1994) the recovery of
payments, made for overstayal ie. service period after the age of
superannuation, will be made from responsible officer instead of the
concerned employee(s).

Scrutiny of the records (March 2013) of the Executive Engineer (EE),
Provincial Division, Lakhimpur Kheri revealed that two employees, Sri Itwari
s/o Sri Chauthi and Sri Prabhu s/o Sri Puttu, were appointed as Beldar (March
1977). Their dates of birth were certified by the Chief Medical Officer (CMO),
Lakhimpur Kheri as 32 years on 17.4.1980 and 16.6.1980 respectively.
Accordingly, they were to retire on 30.04.2008 and 30.06.2008 respectively on
superannuation. However, both the employees were not retired on their due
dates of retirement by the division and both of them continued to draw salary
till September 2011. On the request (September 2011) made by employees for
Voluntary Retirement, it came to notice (October 2011) of the division that
their dates of birth were entered in service book as 17.4.1958 and 16.6.1958
by overwriting on the dates of 17.4.1948 and 16.6.1948 respectively. In the
period of 31 years upto the date of retirement, the department did not carry out
the prescribed checks, verification and correction of the wrong and
overwritten date of birth of employees’ service books, which showed that the
Head of the office viz. EE did not comply with the provisions of financial
tules regarding service books. This led to overstayal in service by these
employees by 39 and 41 months respectively, resulting in payment of
%10.36 lakh as pay and allowances. They were however retired retrospectively
from their due dates of retirement (30.4.2008 and 30.6.2008 respectively) on
22.10.2011 by EE. An enquiry committee, formed (October 2011) and later
reconstituted (September 2012) by EE, held responsible, in it’s report,
establishment clerks for manipulation in service books.

On this being pointed in audit, EE stated (March 2013) that report of inquiry
committee is awaited and action will be taken as per the direction of higher
authorities. Further, EE communicated (September 2013) that enquiry has
been completed and overpayment was recovered from the concerned officials.
The reply confirms the facts. Thus, non-adherence to the provisions of
financial rules regarding necessary checks of service books and system failure
resulted in overstayal of Beldars in service and over payment to them as pay
and allowances. Further, recovery was made (July to September 2013) from
the two beldars, (when the matter was pointed in the audit in March 2013),
against the recovery to be made by the department from the responsible
officer(s) as required under the Government Order (October 1994). Further, no
action had been initiated against the erring officials.

The Government, during discussion (January 2014), confirmed the facts and
figures and stated that the amount of overpayment was recovered from the
concerned officials and action was being taken against the erring officials/
officers.

Audit Report (Gener:

3.1.3, Suspected fraud and misappropriation

‘Payment of & 4.31 crore in violation of the financial rules, accounting
system and Government orders led to fraud and misappropriation.

As per provisions of Financial Hand Book Vol.-VI‘, Government order (July
1991) and Circular (July 1991) of Engineer-in-Chief, Public Works
Department, no drawls from treasury were to be made without bills; daily
transactions were to be entered in the cash book and verified regularly.
Disbursing officers are expected to tally each entry of payment in the cash
book against vouchers and the entries of cheques entered in cheque issue
register must be verified. Disbursement of payments is to be made only after
these checks have been exercised. Works abstract posted with the vouchers
must be completed daily, tallied with cash outlay and the monthly account
must be prepared factoring in the works abstract and counter checked with
entries in cash book. Monthly account, containing classified abstract of
expenditure and schedule of works expenditure, must be sent to the office of
Accountant General every month. Divisional Officer will, before submitting
the monthly accounts, carefully examine the books, returns and records from
which these are compiled. The divisional officer is responsible for the
correctness, in all respect, of the original records of cash and stores; receipts
and expenditure; and the completeness of vouchers.

Scrutiny of the records (February 2013) of Executive Engineer (EE),
Provincial Division, PWD, Amethi, revealed that during September, October,
November, December 2012 and January 2013 ten cheques aggregating
% 4.31 crore® were issued to a contractor M/s Hind Construction Enterprises
which were encashed during September 2012 to January 2013, in the
respective months of the issue of these cheques, without supporting vouchers
and entries in the cash book and cheque issue register. Cheques of preceding
and succeeding numbers were however, entered in the cash book. Monthly
closing of cash book was not done since September 2012 till 18 February
2013. The monthly accounts of these months were prepared and sent to the
Office of the Accountant General (A&E) excluding the details of drawls/
payments of हैं 4.31 crore. It was further found that the payments against these
cheques were included in the accounts for March 2013 through transfer entry
order (TEO) by adjusting minus balances of the amounts shown in Form-51 of
February 2013. Adjustment through TEO was not in order as drawals of minus
cheques, shown in TEO, were not as per financial rules. These were debited as
miscellaneous advance against the contractor. As such, the accounting system,
financial rules and regulations, the Government Orders and departmental
circulars were violated by making fraudulent payment of % 4.31 crore to
favour a contractor. Entry of the issue of the cheques was not made in the
cheque issue register, cash book and works abstract. Monthly account
(February 2013) was prepared and sent (March 2013) to the Accountant

On this being pointed out in audit, the then EE stated in his reply (February

figures and stated that the enquiry report against the concerned EE was
awaited. Necessary action would be taken as per rules on receipt of the
enquiry report.

3.2 Non-compliance with rules and regulations

For sound financial administration and financial control it is essential that
expenditure conforms to financial rules, regulations and orders passed by the
competent authority. This not only prevents irregularities, misappropriation
and frauds, but helps in maintaining good financial discipline. Some of the
audit findings on non-compliance with rules and regulations are hereunder.

HOME DEPARTMENT (POLICE)

3.2.1 Non-observation of the prescribed purchase procedure led to excess
expenditure

‘As the prescribed purchase procedure was not followed, the
Government was deprived of the benefit of competitive prices. Further,
the violation of the principles of Financial Discipline and Management
resulted in excess expenditure of € 1.40 crore.

As per the Chapter XIX of the Budget Manual of the State Government
relating to the Principle of Financial Discipline and Management, every public
officer is expected to exercise the same vigilance in respect of expenditure
from public moneys as a person of ordinary prudence would exercise in
respect of expenditure of his own money. Further, the Finance Department
also issued an order in September 2008 for mandatory invitation of tenders for
purchase of material valued above & one lakh.

ee)


Audit Report (Gener:

Scrutiny of the records in Central Audit of vouchers at Accountant General
(A&E) office and further information collected (December 2012) from the
office of the Additional Director General of Police, Police Headquarters
(ADGP, PHQ), Allahabad revealed (November 2012) that the Government, to
control riots in the State after the judgment (30 September 2010) of the
Hon’ble High Court on Ram Janam Bhumi and Babri Masjid, sanctioned
(August 2010) % four crore for purchase of 80,000 pieces of torch® for use by
the homeguard volunteers with the condition that these would be purchased in
accordance with the prescribed procedure. However, ADGP, PHQ, Allahabad
ordered (September 2010) to purchase the torches from the Uttar Pradesh
Rajya Karamchari Kalayan Nigam, despite the fact that this was not one of
the reserved items authorised for purchase from it, without inviting
quotations/tenders in contravention of the aforesaid order (September 2008) of
the Finance Department. This deprived the Government of the benefit of
competitive prices. The purchase orders were accordingly, issued (September
2010) to the Nigam. However, before the supplies of the torches commenced,
the law and order situation of the State changed and the Principal Secretary,
Homeguard, Government of UP, intimated (October 2010) ADGP, PHQ,
Allahabad that the purchase of such a large number of torches were not
required in view of the changed circumstances. Instead of cancelling the
aforesaid purchase order, ADGP, PHQ, Allahabad received the supplies
(January 2011 to March 2011) from the supplier which were arranged through
a private firm’ and paid & 2.80 crore (February 2011 and March 2011). The
remaining amount of हैं 1.20 crore was surrendered (March 2011) to the
Government. The torches purchased were allotted (March 2011) to the District
Commandants, Homeguards.

Audit compared the market price of the torches and also ascertained their end
use. It revealed that the market price of the torch per piece was lower than the
rate at which the Nigam had supplied®. This led to excess expenditure of
₹ 1.40 crore. Further, in one district? where 2,200 torches were delivered,
1,200 were made available to the police stations for use by police and only the
balance 1,000 to the District Commandant, Homeguards for homeguard
volunteers. In nine districts!°, where 17,015 torches were supplied, 13,280
were lying in stock'! as of May 2013 since March 2011 (when these were
delivered).

whom these were not purchased and were also lying unused in the offices of
the District Commandants.

Special Secretary, Government of UP stated (April 2013) that the torches were
required urgently to control riots in the State. The reply was not acceptable
because the supplies of torch were received quite sometime after the judgment
and after the intimation (October 2010) of Principal Secretary, Homeguard,

view of the changed circumstances.

The matter was referred to the Government in May 2013. However, reply has
not been received (February 2014).

HOUSING AND URBAN PLANNING DEPARTMENT

3.2.2 Retention of large funds outside the Government Accounts

Failure of the Government to observe codal provisions led to retention of
रै 233.12 crore outside the Government account in a scheduled bank for
over 36 months already.

funds from the treasuries unless required for immediate use.

a monument’? in Lucknow out of the interests earned on the amounts and

As of June 2013, % 233.12 crore, lying in the Corpus Fund, was invested in the
Term Deposit Receipt/Flexi Account for one year to earn an interest of
& 21.52 crore upto May 2014.

In reply, the Government stated (October 2013) that the amounts were retained
in the banks after obtaining consent of the Finance Department to earn
maximum interest. The reply confirms that the funds were not required for
immediate use. During discussion (February 2014), the Government,
confirming the facts and figures, stated that further action will be taken after
consultation with the Finance Department.

Thus, the Government, in disregard of the codal provisions, retained
रू 233.12 crore, for more than 36 months already, outside the Government
account in bank in the form of Term Deposit Receipt. Further, frequent
changes in the decisions by the Government with regard to source of funding
for maintenance of the monuments and ultimately adopting the budgetary
route was indicative of lack of decisiveness on the part of the Government.

3.2.3. Undue favour to the contractors

Non-compliance to the Government notification for deduction of Cess by
the Head Works Division, Agra Canal, resulted in undue financial aid of
= 1.26 crore to the contractors.

With a view to regulate the employment and conditions of service of building
and other construction workers and to provide for their safety, health and
welfare the Government of India (Gol) enacted ‘The Building and Other
Construction Workers Welfare Cess Act, 1996' which came into force on the
1 March 1996. The Government of Uttar Pradesh (GoUP) also implemented the
Act vide a notification!® (February 2009) and subsequently constituted”?
(November 2009) UP Building and other Construction Workers Welfare
Board (Board) in compliance to Gol’s Act. Further, GoUP issued (November

* Maintenance works: € 2.93 crore; Salaries and wages: & 27.86 lakh; Electricity: हैं. 1.20 crore; Decoration:

Notification No, 143 dated 04.02.2009.

* Notification No.1411 dated 20.11.2009.

2009) instructions”! to all executive agencies for deduction of Cess at the rate
of one per cent of the total cost of the construction incurred by an employer as
envisaged in the Act. Accordingly, all the Government departments and the
public sector undertakings, engaged in construction works, were required to
deduct Cess at the rate of one per cent from the bills of the contractors and
deposit the same to the Board through a crossed demand draft payable in
favour of "UP Buildings and other construction workers Welfare Board".
Subsequently, the Chief Secretary, GoUP also issued directions (February
2010) to all the departments for compliance of the Government notification of
February 2009.

During scrutiny of the records (June 2012) of the Executive Engineer (EE),
Head Works Division, Agra Canal, Okhla, New Delhi, Irrigation Department
U.P. we observed that the Superintending Engineer, Executive Engineer and
Assistant Engineers executed 939” agreements amounting to = 160.97 crore
during the period April 2010 to May 2012 for various works. They paid
% 168.20” crore (May 2013) to the contractors, but deduction on account of
Cess amounting to % 1.68% crore (one per cent) as envisaged in the
notification, was not made. Amount of % 0.42 crore was however recovered
from the bills of contractors after the issue was raised in audit (May 2013).

On this being pointed out in audit EE stated (June 2012) that as the division
was not aware of the Government Order relating to deduction of Cess, it did
not make provisions for the same in the approved estimates. However, it was
seen in audit that the Government Order for deduction of Cess was received in
division on 10 December 2010. Thus, non-compliance to the Government
notification in respect of deduction of Cess, applicable in all the States’ and
the Central Government departments or of a Public Sector Undertaking,
resulted in undue financial aid of € 1.26 crore to the contractors.

The Government, during discussion (November 2013), while accepting the
facts and figures, stated that action would be ensured against the concerned.
officers by fixing the responsibility and direction was given to recover the
balance labour Cess from the contractors.

clause relating to levy of centage charges in the MoU, signed between Chief

decision of the higher authorities and conditions of MoU, centage charges
were not levied. It was further stated that both the departments were under the
State Government. The reply was not correct as NOIDA claims to be a self-
financed autonomous body, not receiving any grants from the Government and
all senior positions in it are manned by senior Government officers. Moreover,
as per the Government orders (February 1997 and January 2011) centage
charges @ 12.5 per cent on deposit works, carried out by Public
Works/Irrigation Departments for the works of non-Government organisation
and local bodies are to be levied and therefore, centage charge should have
been levied as envisaged in the financial rules and Government orders.

The Government, during discussion (November 2013), while accepting the
facts and figures, stated that action would be ensured against the concerned
officers by fixing their responsibility and direction was given to recover the
centage charges from NOIDA authority.

Thus, non-adherence to the financial rules and Government orders had already
led to loss of % 13.22 crore” to the Government due to non-levy of centage
charges till April 2013.It would increase further as expenditure progresses.

3.2.5 Loss to the Government

Purchase of high density polyethylene/polypropylene bags, in violation of
financial rules and the Government orders, led to loss of हैं 1.18 crore to
the Government.

Para 523 of Financial Hand Book Vol VI envisages that to facilitate the
preparation of estimates a Schedule of Rates (SoR) for each kind of work,
commonly executed, should be maintained and kept up to date. The rates
mentioned in the estimates should generally agree with the scheduled rates.
Further, the Government orders* stipulate that Superintending Engineer (SE)
is authorised to purchase materials only up to % one lakh in a calendar month
on the basis of quotation/tender. For any procurement above % one lakh,
invitation of tenders is mandatory. The Government order (October 2007) also
provided that purchases ranging from % 1.5 crore to रैं six crore be made only
through Departmental Purchase Committee.

Scrutiny of the records (November 2012) of the Executive Engineer (E
Flood Division, Badaun revealed that 28 lakh new HDPE/PP bags were to be
urchased for two flood protection projects’. We observed that these
bags were purchased by the Superintending Engineer, Flood Works Circle,
Bareilly (SE) in May and June 2012 at = 2.64 crore beyond his financial
powers for purchase of materials. Of the seven supply orders placed for the
ags, six were on a single firm*’. No tenders were invited by SE and the
urchase was made on the basis of quotations in violation of Government
orders. Moreover, during the scrutiny of sanctioned estimates® of these
projects and Schedule of Rates (SoR) prescribed for Badaun district, it was
found that against the rate of हैं 5.20 per bag, supply orders for the bags
were placed @ % 9.40 to हैं 9.47 per bag, which was higher by हैं 4.20 to
र 4.27 per bag. The division paid हैं 2.64 crore (May 2013) for supply of 27.98
akh bags. Thus, placing supply orders, beyond financial powers and without
inviting tenders, at higher rates by the SE, in violation of the Government
orders and financial rules, led to loss of हैं 1.18 crore to the Government
( Appendix-3.2).

On this being pointed out in audit, EE stated (November 2012) that the
quotations were invited by SE after the approval of Chief Engineer (East
Ganga) Irrigation Department, UP, Moradabad and supply orders were placed
at the minimum rates. The reply confirms that the supply orders were placed
on quotation basis by SE, beyond his financial limit in a calendar month and
also at rates higher than those prescribed in SoR, without inviting tenders, for
purchase. Further, the procurement was also not made through the required
Departmental Purchase Committee, as envisaged in the Government order for
procurements between % 1.50 crore to हैं 6.00 crore.

3.2.6 Wasteful expenditure

Construction of dampeners without ensuring technical soundness,|
resulted in breach of bund, wasteful expenditure and creation of liability
of हैं 1.35 crore.

Para 318 and 375 of Financial Rules provide that no work shall be
commenced unless a properly detailed design and estimate have been
sanctioned and allotment of funds made, and orders for its commencement
issued by the competent authority. Further, as per Para 318 of Financial Rules,
technical sanction must be obtained before a work is commenced. These
approvals ensure that the works are structurally sound and the estimates are
accurately calculated and based on adequate data. The Government order
(August 2011) also provides that it is essential to obtain Administrative
Approval (AA), Financial and Technical Sanction (F&TS) and to ensure
availability of funds before commencement of any work.

Scrutiny of the records (October 2012) of the Executive Engineer (EE), Flood
Works Division, Gonda revealed that with a view to protect Elgin Bridge
Charsari bundh (in Gonda) from Km 11.530 to Km 12.800 and a spur at Km
11.750, the Permanent Steering Committee (PSC) of Uttar Pradesh State Flood
Control Council accorded (July 2011) approval to a project for construction of
four dampeners*> at a cost of % 1.39 crore. The EE, however, executed four
agreements*® for construction of dampeners*’ with scheduled dates of
commencement as 22 May 2011 and completion as 21 June 2011 at a cost of
% 0.26 crore even before the approval of the project by PSC and without
obtaining AA, F&TS from the competent authority. Besides, the estimate of
the project was also not sanctioned by the Chief Engineer (CE). The work
was completed (June 2011) at a cost of € 1.35 crore*® including material of
र 1.05 crore issued from stock. The dampeners were submerged and washed
away in the river (August 2011) during flood resulting in breach of bundh at
Km 11.560 and damage of spur”. Though the division did not make any
payment (August 2013) and showed the expenditure as liability, construction
of dampeners without technical sanction, in violation of para 318 and
375 of financial rules and the Government order (August 2011), led to breach
of bundh resulting in wasteful expenditure and creation of liability of
रू 1.35 crore due to non-availability of funds.

On this being pointed out in audit (October 2012 and February 2013), the EE
stated that the project was approved by the Permanent Steering Committee and

Irregular drawl of = 24.31 crore and retaining the same in the form of

resulted in the amount being unavailable to the Government.

responsibility of the controlling officer to surrender appropriations or portions
thereof which are not likely to be required during the year as soon as lapses or
savings are foreseen. Further, as per para 94 of Financial Hand book Vol.-VI,
the divisional officer (Executive Engineer), as the primary disbursing officer
of the division, is responsible not only for the financial regularity of the

3.2.8 Avoidable expenditure

According technical sanction and execution of work with costlier
specifications against the norms laid down by PWD for construction of
rural road led to avoidable expenditure of € 1.84 crore.

As per circularof August 2007 of the Public Works Department (PWD),
Uttar Pradesh for construction of new rural link roads, the crust thickness of
tural roads was to be kept at 25 cm with surface painting of 20 mm by premix
carpeting (PC) with seal coat type —A’ over the crust.

Scrutiny of the records (November 2009 and October 2010) of the Executive
Engineer (EE), Irrigation Division, Bijnore and further information collected
(May 2012 and February 2013) revealed that the Expenditure Finance
Committee accorded approval (January 2008) for construction of metalled
road on left bank of Nehtaur Branch of Eastern Ganga Canal from Km 0.000
to Km 43.346 at 14.80 crore in district Bijnore. The Government accorded
financial sanction to the project in January 2009. The technical sanction (TS)
was accorded (February 2007) by the Chief Engineer (CE), East Ganga,
Irrigation Department, Uttar Pradesh, Moradabad with provisions of soling

Borrowings and rate of interest of 2012-13 has been assumed same as the borrowings and rate of interest of year
2011-12, as per the Report on State Finances for the year ended 2011-12.

coat, intermediate coat, top coat each of stone ballast (10 cm) and Bituminous
Macadam (BM-5 cm), Semi Dense Bituminous Concrete (SDBC- 2.5 cm) as
bituminous surface for the construction of the road, which was costlier and
against the norms laid down in the PWD circular for rural road. The
Superintending Engineer, East Ganga Canal Construction Circle, Haridwar
executed four agreements “(February 2009) for $12.86 crore** on short term
tender notice with scheduled date of commencement as 20 February 2009 and

(July 2013). It was also seen that the work was incomplete (July 2013) against

down by the PWD for construction of rural road has already led to avoidable
expenditure of = 1.84’ crore as of July 2013, which is likely to increase as
expenditure/completion progresses.

On this being pointed out, CE stated (May 2011) that the crust thickness of the
road was kept at 25 cm as per the PWD circular of August 2007 and the work
of BM and SDBC was done as per para 1.2.3 of the Government order of
December 2003**. The reply was not acceptable as the TS accorded by the CE
was against the PWD circular of August 2007, which prescribed that the
surface painting of 20 mm by PC with seal coat type -‘A’ was to be done on
the crust thickness of 25 cm. Besides, the Government order of December
2003 pertains to strengthening and widening of State highway, major and
other district roads and not the rural roads.

The Government, during discussion (November 2013), while accepting the
facts and figures, stated that the responsibility, after enquiry, would be fixed
for undertaking the work on short term tender basis. In future work should be
done after completing all the norms and as per specifications. Necessary action
would be taken after enquiry for executing the above work in violation of
norms of PWD and IRC.

Chapter 3 - Audit of Transactions

3.2.10 Avoidable expenditure

Deviation from sanctioned provisions for construction of a road resulted
in avoidable expenditure of हैं 1.58 crore.

Paragraph 381 of Financial Hand Book Vol-V1 provides that no material
alteration in sanctioned designs may be made by a divisional officer in
carrying out any work, without the approval of the superintending engineer. In
the case of material modifications of or deviations from a sanctioned estimate
it is the duty of the executive officers to see that the sanction of the competent
authority is obtained.

During audit we observed that the Government accorded (January 2009)
administrative approval and financial sanction of हैं 92.56 crore, under State
road fund, for widening and strengthening of Balia-Garwar-Nagara-Barauli
road, Ballia in Km 1 to 61. The Chief Engineer (CE), Azamgarh Zone, PWD,
Azamgath accorded (February 2009) technical sanction for the same amount.
The existing crust of the road from km 1 to 36 and km 37 to 61 was, 400 mm
and 300 mm respectively, which was to be strengthened by increasing the
thickness of the crust to 680 mm (Appendix-3.5) which included 150 mm
thick layer of Wet Mix Macadam (WMM) with other required layers.

Further, during scrutiny (June 2011) of the records of the Executive Engineer
(EE), Provincial Division, Ballia, we observed that the Superintending
Engineer, Ballia Circle, Ballia executed (February 2009) a contract bond
for = 59.06 crore with M/s Chhatrashakti Construction Company. The work
was scheduled to be commenced and completed by 25 February 2009 and
24 February 2011 respectively. Payments of % 54.60 crore’ had been made to
the contractor (September 2013). We also observed that an additional layer of
100 mm of WMM over the sanctioned 150 mm WMM was laid without the
sanction of the competent authority in stretches of km 05, 06, 13-20, 23,
36 and 37 on the existing and widened portions of the road, which resulted in
the increase of total thickness of crust to 780 mm (Appendix-3.5) in these
stretches against the sanctioned crust of 680 mm. Resultantly, avoidable
expenditure of = 1.58 crore was incurred on execution of extra quantity of
7,100 cum® of 100 mm additional layer of WMM as of June 2013.

On this being pointed out in audit, EE stated (April 2012) that additional
material of WMM was laid in these stretches for profile and camber correction
as the additional work was absolutely necessary to avoid deterioration of the
road due to water logging, the expenditure was met out of the saving under the
project and the expenditure will be kept under the sanctioned budget. The
reply was not correct as the sanctioned crust thickness of 680 mm was to be
achieved by 150 mm of WMM layer with other layers. Laying of the

not sanctioned by the competent authority as it resulted in the increase of the
total crust from sanctioned 680 mm to 780 mm. Thus, an avoidable

3.2.11 Avoidable expenditure

Use of costlier specification, in contravention of IRC guidelines, resulted
in avoidable expenditure of = one crore on widening and strengthening of
Padampur-Khamriya link road in Ambedakar Nagar.

The specifications of IRC- 37-2001 provide guidelines for pavement design of
the road given the cumulative traffic (msa), CBR™ etc, Further, E- in- C, PWD
also issued directions (September 2008) for adopting IRC specifications while
framing the estimates for construction of roads.

We, during audit, observed that the Government accorded (January 2008)
Administrative Approval (AA) and Financial Sanction (FS) of ¢ 3.82 crore for
the widening and strengthening of Padampur-Khamriya link road (km1 to
7.20) in Ambedakar Nagar. Technical Sanction (TS) for the work was
accorded (April 2008) by CE, Faizabad Zone, PWD, Faizabad for % 3.82 crore,
which was subsequently revised and sanctioned (June 2010) for ₹ 4.47 crore.
SE, Faizabad Circle, PWD, Faizabad executed (May 2008) a Contract Bond
(cB)* of % 2.26 crore for the work of km. 1 to km. 5.50. The work for
remaining length of 1.70 km (km 5.50 to 7.20) was also got executed by the
department later through the same contractor on the agreed rates of the CB.

Scrutiny of the records of EE, Construction Division-II, PWD, Ambedakar
Nagar revealed (June 2011) that CBR value of sub-grade of the road was
6 per cent; msa was 0.91 (say 1) and the crust design sanctioned was 450
mm”, which included costlier bituminous layers of Bituminous Macadam
(BM) and Semi-Dense Bituminous Macadam (SDBC) in 7.20 km length of the
said road whereas the required crust of road should be 390 mm“ including the
less costlier bituminous layer of Premix carpet as per the provisions of
IRC-37-2001. Thus, = 1.62 crore was spent as of January 2014 for laying of
costlier bituminous layer, which led to avoidable expenditure of = one crore
(Appendix-3.6) due to faulty sanction of the crust in the estimate and its
execution.

On this being pointed out in audit, EE replied that the work was done as per
requirement of traffic of the road and according to TS accorded. The reply was
not acceptable as TS was not in accordance with IRC specifications and
utilization of costlier bituminous specification for the construction of the road
resulted in an avoidable expenditure of % one crore.

3.2.12 Avoidable expenditure

Incorrect adoption of existing crust thickness in the specifications for
widening and strengthening of Lucknow-Ballia road, resulted in
avoidable expenditure of € 1.56 crore.

Paragraph 318 of Financial Hand Book, VOL VI stipulates that technical
sanction to the estimate ensures that the proposals are structurally sound and
the estimates are accurately calculated and based on adequate data.

Scrutiny of the records (March 2011) of Executive Engineer (EE), Provincial
Division (PD), Public Works Department (PWD), Jaunpur revealed that the
Government accorded administrative and financial sanction (February 2008)
of हैं 27.95 crore for widening and strengthening of Lucknow-Ballia State
Highway (km 187 to 216; total 29 km) in Jaunpur. The Chief Engineer (CE),
Azamgarh Zone, Azamgarh accorded technical sanction to the detailed
estimate of this work for the same amount in February 2009. Technical
sanction contained the provision for increasing the thickness of the crust of the
existing road from 17.50 cm to 70 cm" .The crust of the existing road was
computed as 17.50 cm in place of 23 cm, by taking into account 12 cm stone
ballast layer and only 5.50 cm kankar layer, half of 11.00 cm existing layer of
kankar. The Superintending Engineer, Azamgarh Circle, Azamgarh executed
Contract Bonds” (February 2009 & December 2009) for executing the said
work. This incorrect estimation of the existing thickness of 17.50 cm of the
crust, instead of the existing crust thickness of 23 cm, led to avoidable
expenditure of हैं 1.56 crore due to laying of excess layer of 5.5 cm of wet
mix macadam, which increased the total crust thickness to 75.50 cm in place
of the sanctioned 70 cm. (Appendix-3.7). The graphic presentation of
pavement composition of the road is as under:

3.3 Audit against propriety and cases of expenditure without adequate

justification

Authorisation of expenditure from public funds has to be guided by the
principles of propriety and efficiency of public expenditure. Authorities
empowered to incur expenditure are expected to enforce the same vigilance as
a person of ordinary prudence would exercise in respect of his own money and
should enforce financial order and strict economy at every step. Audit has
detected instances of impropriety and extra expenditure.

AGRICULTURE DEPARTMENT

3.3.1 Undue aid to an insurance company

Failure of the Government to withdraw its share as per scheme
guidelines with effect from 2005-06 by determining premium on
actuarial basis, led to the Government paying हैं 149 crore (2008-12) as
farmers’ share towards premium (€ 20 crore) and insurance claim
(₹ 129 crore) to an insurance company under National Agriculture
Insurance Scheme.

To provide financial support to the small and marginal farmers in the event of
failure of their crop yields (rabi and kharif) due to natural calamities ete, Gol
launched (July 1999) National Agriculture Insurance Scheme“ (NAIS). As per
Para 3 of the guidelines of the scheme, initially the farmers were to pay
50 per cent of the premium to the insurance company® and Gol and the State
Government were to share equally remaining 50 per cent. However, the share
of Gol and the State Government was to be phased out in five years (i.e. by
2004-05) by determining the premium payable on actuarial basis. Till then, in
terms of the Para 8 (Ka) of the guidelines, the insurance company was to bear
claims upto 100 per cent of the premium. Gol and the State Government were
to share the liability equally the claims above 100 per cent of the premium.

Natural fire and lighting, storms, hailstorms, cyclone, typhoon, floods and inundation landslides, drought, dry
spells, pests/diseases etc,

The scheme envisages coverage of all food crops (cereals, millets and pulses), oil seeds and annual
commercial/horticultural crops.

Agriculture Insurance Company of India, Limited is the implementing agency in respect of the State of Uttar
Pradesh,

O— न््नन्‍नन्‍नननीनीीनणन«»--


Chapter 3 - Audit of Transactions

With effect from 2005-06, the insurance company was to bear claims upto
150 per cent of the premium and Gol] and the State Government were to share
the liability of the claims above 150 per cent equally.

The Government sanctioned % 140.18 crore during 2008-12 for payment of
its share of subsidy in premium and insurance claim to the insurance company.
The amounts were drawn from the treasury and paid (2008-12) to the
insurance company.

Scrutiny of the records of the office of the Director, Agriculture, UP, Lucknow
revealed (March 2013) that Gol and the State Government phased out their
shares from 50 per cent to 10 per cent by 2005-06 and continued their share of
premium at 10 per cent during the period from 2005-06 to 2011-12. Scrutiny
further revealed (March 2013) that the small and marginal farmers” got their
lands insured during 2008-12 for the crop yields against the natural
calamities, pests etc. for हैं 15,363 crore under NAIS. The premium of
र 309 crore was payable to the insurance company by the farmers during
2008-12. As the Government continued their share of premium at 10 per cent
during the period from 2005-06 to 2011-12, % 20 crore was paid by the
Government (Gol: हैं 9.97 crore and State Government: हैं 9.97 crore) during
the same period.

Further, small and marginal farmers” presented claims of & 361.04 crore to
the insurance company during 2008-12 for failure of the crop yields (rabi and
kharif) and accordingly the insurance company was to bear the financial
liability of ¥ 361.04 crore in terms of the Para 8 (Ka) of the scheme guidelines.
However, out of ~ 361.04 crore, the Government shared the claims of
₹ 128.66 crore (Gol: हैं 64.33 crore and the State Government: % 64.33 crore)
and the insurance company paid only = 232.38 crore.

Thus, the Government provided undue aid of = 149 crore (premium: % 20 crore
and claims: = 128.66 crore) to the insurance company under National
Agriculture Insurance Scheme during 2008-12 due to failure to withdraw its
share in premium and claims with effect from 2005-06 by determining the
premium payable on actuarial basis. This extra burden of हैं 149 crore was
because the Government failed to phase out its contribution as per the scheme
guidelines.

The Director, Agriculture, UP, Lucknow admitted (June 2013) that the
premium payable to the insurance company on insurance of crop yields was
not determined on actuarial basis. During discussion (December 2013), the
Government confirmed the facts and figures and admitted that the share of Gol
and the State Government was not phased out in five years.

HIGHER EDUCATION DEPARTMENT

3.3.2. Unfruitful expenditure

Expenditure of = 3.84 crore was rendered unfruitful as the requisite
technical approval of the detailed drawings and designs was not
obtained prior to the commencement of construction of a swimming
pool in premises of the Chattrapati Shahuji Maharaj University,
Kanpur. The swimming pool was lying incomplete (August 2013).

As per Para 375 of the Financial Handbook (Volume VI), no work shall
commenced unless detailed drawings and designs have been sanctioned by 1
competent authority.

oo

We during scrutiny (July 2012) of the records of the Finance Officer,
Chattrapati Shahuji Maharaj University, Kanpur (University) observed that
the Building Construction Committee of the University, under the
chairmanship of the Vice Chancellor (VC), sanctioned construction of a
swimming pool at a total cost of 4.88 crore” between January 2004 and
January 2007 for execution by the Uttar Pradesh Rajkiya Nirman Nigam
Limited (UPRNN) and released’! (2005-08) ₹ 3.84 crore thereagainst without
prescribing the date of completion. UPRNN commenced (June 2005) the
construction works without technical approval of the detailed drawings and.
designs by the Managing Director, UPRNN, who was the competent authority.
When the construction was in progress, a committee” of the University
inspected (March 2006) the site and noticed flaws such as the direction of the
shallow and deep ends of the swimming pool were wrong. Instead of
initiating any action for rectification, the works were continued till January
2007, by when the entire sanctioned and released amount of & 3.84 crore was
spent. After four years of observing the flaws, the Swimming Pool Inspection
Committee, constituted by VC, visited (April 2010) the site and further
observed that the direction of the swimming pool was incorrect and free space
on the deck around both the ends of the pool was insufficient, in addition to
non-finalisation of the filtration plant such as specification, location etc. As of
August 2013, the swimming pool was lying incomplete. Thus, commencement
of the works without technical approval, as required under para 375 of the
Financial Handbook, resulted in unfruitful expenditure of = 3.84 crore.

In reply, the Government stated (June 2013) that two committees were
constituted for rectifications of the defects. Further during discussion
(August 2013), while accepting the facts and figures, it stated that reply to the
audit observations would be sent on receipt of replies to the explanations being
called for from the officers of UPRNN and the University.

™ Swimming pool (% 3.64 crore) in January 2004, a boundary wall (% 38.02 lakh) in January 2006 and external
development works (€ 85.74 lakh) in January 2007.

IRRIGATION DEPARTMENT

3.3.3 Excess expenditure

Change in the specifications of lining, without techno-economic viability!
and the stipulated prior approval of Central Water Commission and
planning Commission of India, in the main canal of Madhya Ganga Canal}
Project (Stage -ID), led to excess expenditure of T 120.16 crore.

The Central Water Commission (CWC), Government of India accorded (June
2007) approval to the Madhya Ganga Canal Project (Stage-II) (Project) after
undertaking the techno-economic viability. The Project aimed at utilising the
monsoon discharge of river Ganga by diverting its water through existing left
bank head regulator of Madhya Ganga Barrage in Bijnore, Uttar Pradesh. The
project was listed and financed under Accelerated Irrigation Benefit
Programme (AIBP). The administrative approval and financial sanction for the
project was accorded (July 2007) at a cost of हैं 1,060.76 crore by the
Government of Uttar Pradesh (UP). The Planning Commission and Water
Resources Division, Government of India issued investment clearance
(December 2008) for the project at an estimated cost of हैं 1,060.76 crore.
Further, the Planning Commission directed that “the State Finance Department
would restrict the expenditure to the approved cost and no additional
expenditure beyond approved cost may be permitted unless the revised
estimate is got approved”. The project envisaged the construction of 66.20 km
long main canal with double brick lining on the slopes and single brick lining
on the bed with relief valves in case of main canal, as the brick lining
was found to be economical. The project as scheduled to be completed
during 2011-12.

० a Canal ss / fo Lining

Bed

Graphic presentation of lining of canal

Scrutiny of the records (February 2011 & October 2012) of the Executive
Engineer (EE), Madhya Ganga Canal Construction (MGCC) Division-I,
Hardwar revealed that the construction of main canal from Km. 20.000 to
31.550 was entrusted to this division. It was observed that the slopes of
the main canal were lined by cement concrete (CC) instead of bricks which
was 2.7 times costlier. Further, information collected (March 2012 and
November 2012) from the Chief Engineer, Madhya Ganga Canal Project,
Irrigation Department U.P. Aligarh (CE), revealed that CC lining was done in
the whole project without ascertaining the techno-economic viability and
obtaining the required prior approval of CWC and the Planning Commission.
The brick lining of 2,773 thousand sq. meter of the main canal, approved at

ep


dit Report (General y ended 31 March 2013

the rate of € 425.06 per sq. meter” for = 117.87 crore was instead replaced by
CC lining of 1,702.03 thousand sq. meter for हैं 192.50 crore till March 2013.
The brick lining, as approved, for the same executed quantity would have
been executed at a cost of = 72.34 crore™, Thus, an excess expenditure of
% 120.16 crore” had already been incurred till March 2013.

On this being pointed out in audit, EE stated (February 2011) that the work of
CC lining was being executed as per the estimates sanctioned by the higher
authorities. He further stated (October 2012) that CC tile lining was found
suitable in place of brick lining in compliance to the order of the Government of
U.P. of July 2005, and IS Code 10430-1982. The provision of CC tile lining in
place of brick lining was made at the level of Chief Engineer, based on which
the revised estimate was prepared. Subsequently, CE communicated (June
2013) that the revised project has been sanctioned for CC lining by CWC.
However, the enclosure attached with investment clearance (June 2013) of the
Planning Commission used the term “brick lining”. Further, no justification was
furnished either by CE or mentioned in the revised project report for tile lining
against the prescribed brick lining, which was reckoned techno-economically
viable by CWC in 2007. Moreover, the department proposed and received the
approval for brick lining in July 2007 after the issue of the order of July 2005,
referred to by the department in support of the CC lining. Further, the Planning
Commission, while according the investment clearance for the project had
specifically instructed that no additional expenditure beyond the approved cost
would be permitted unless the revised estimate was approved. This was,
however not adhered to and excess expenditure of € 120.16 crore was incurred
without the stipulated prior approval of Planning Commission (for revision in
sanctioned costs) and CWC (for technical changes). Moreover, as per the
provisions of IS 10430: 2000, life expectancy of concrete, brick/tile lining is
assumed to be the same”, Revised sanction was not only against the basic
criterion of techno-economic viability adopted by CWC, while according the
original sanction but also in violation of its direction that no additional
expenditure, beyond the approved cost would be admissible unless approved by
the Planning Commission and CWC.

The Government, during discussion (November 2013), while accepting the
facts and figures, stated that if change in specifications of lining has been
done, action would be ensured against the concerned officers.

3.3.5 Loss to the Government

Dismantling of pan shaped stone ja/i just after it’s fixing resulted in loss of
र₹ 0.83 crore to the Government and misutilization of funds to the tune of|
X 1.98 crore.

Paragraph 21 (i) of General Financial Rules stipulates that every Government
servant should exercise the same vigilance and care in respect of expenditure
from public money under his control as 3 person of ordinary prudence would
exercise in respect of expenditure of his own money. Further, fund under State
Road Fund (SRF) shall be utilised for maintenance, repair and strengthening
of roads, under SRF rules, 2000.

Scrutiny and information collected (June 2012 and May 2013) of the records
of Executive Engineer (EE), Provincial Division (PD), Public Works
Department (PWD), Lucknow revealed that the Government sanctioned
(February 2011) ₹ 6.36 crore under State Road Fund (SRF) for supply and
fixing of pan shaped stone jali as crash barrier on both the sides of Jail road in
Lucknow, parallel to the boundary walls of Ganna Sansthan and Manyawar
Kashiram Smarak (Eco Park). The Chief Engineer (CE), Lucknow accorded
(February 2011) technical sanction (TS) for the same amount. The estimate
included the provision for supply and fixing of stone ja/i; 1800 Running Metre
(RM) on Manyawar Kashiram Smarak (Eco Park) side and 1650 RM on
Ganna Sansthan side of jail road, Lucknow. Superintending Engineer (SE),
Lucknow Circle and EE, PD, Lucknow executed (February 2011) the contract
bonds (CB)** by inviting (June 2010) tenders before the sanction of the work.
The work of providing and fixing of stone ja/i was completed in March 2011
by incurring an expenditure of % 6.68 crore.

Failure of oversight/ governance

The Government has an obligation to improve the quality of life of the people
for which it works towards fulfillment of certain goals in the area of health,
education, development and upgradation of infrastructure and public services
etc. However, Audit noticed instances where funds released by Government
for creating public assets for the benefit of the community remained
unutilised/ blocked and/or proved unfruitful/ unproductive due to
indecisiveness, lack of administrative oversight and concerted action at
various levels. A few cases have been discussed below.

ANIMAL HUSBANDRY DEPARTMENT

3.4.1 Unproductive expenditure

Due to non-synchronization and non-fixing of time-frame for execution
of various items of works for MVAIUs, these were not operational even
after over three years of their sanction, rendering the expenditure of
3.36 crore on the purchase of these vehicles and their fabrication,
unproductive. Besides, the objective of providing door-step veterinary
treatment in the remote rural areas to the beneficiaries was not
achieved.

The Government decided (2009-10) to have 47 Mobile Veterinary and
Artificial Insemination Units (MVAIU) in seven districts®*’ of Bundelkhand
region (cost = 5.64 crore). Each unit was to be equipped with furniture,
equipment / machinery, medicines etc. The objective was to provide veterinary
treatment in remote rural areas at the door-step of the beneficiaries.

Scrutiny of the records of the office of the Director, Animal Husbandry
(Director), Lucknow revealed (April 2012) that the Government, in February
2011, sanctioned हैं 2.35 crore and the Director placed (March 2011) the
purchase order on a firm for 47 Tata power steering vehicles at a cost of = 2.27
crore. However, neither was the purchase of vehicles, their fabrication,
arrangement for medical officers, veterinary officers synchronized nor was a
time-frame fixed for eventual commissioning of MVAIUs. The Director paid
(March 2011) ₹ 2.27 crore (out of & 2.35 crore**) to the firm on pro forma
invoice and the firm supplied these vehicles between May 2011 and July 2011.

Further, the Director invited tenders in June 2011 for fabrication of chassis of
the vehicles. However, the contract for fabrication was not awarded as only
two firms participated in the bid. In August 2011, the tenders were again
invited but the contract for fabrication was again not awarded as the tenderers
were unqualified. In September 2011, tender was invited for the third time and
the contract for fabrication (cost: = 56.78 lakh) was awarded (October 2011)
to a firm®’. The firm fabricated all the 47 chassis and the Director delivered
these to the Chief Veterinary Officers (CVOs) of the seven districts” by
March 2013°!. However, MVAIUs were not made functional, as of July 2013,
due to shortage of medical officers, veterinary officers including drivers; non-
availability of equipment & medicines; non-availability of funds for POL etc.
and the vehicles were used for vaccination camps, carrying rations, sheep
medicines etc. as noticed in test check of records of the concerned CVOs
(Appendix-3.12). Meanwhile, the Government sanctioned = 4.71 crore (April
2011: हैं 3.37 crore and December 2012: % 1.34 crore) for remaining items of
work such as fabrication, furniture, equipment / machinery, medicines etc. but
spent (July 2013) only % 1.09 crore (fabrication: = 56.78 lakh and others: हैं 52

Expenditure of हैं 2.53 crore on newly created veterinary polyclinics at

etc, the Director, Animal Husbandry, Lucknow proposed (November 2003) to

Etawah and at Badot in Bagpat, at = 2.62 crore”’. The Director also proposed
(November 2003) for 37 posts”* of various cadres for these polyclinics. The
Government sanctioned (February 2005) € 2.14 crore (® 1.07 crore each for
the two polyclinics) and assigned (February 2005) the construction of civil
works to the UP Project Corporation Limited (UPPCL) for their completions
(Safai: July 2007 and Badot: May 2010).

Scrutiny of the records of the Director, Animal Husbandry, Lucknow
(Director) revealed (May 2013) that he released हैं 2.14 crore to UPPCL in
March 2005 and made the required land available (Safai in District Etawah:
February 2005; and Badot in District Bagpat: December 2005). UPPCL
commenced (October 2005) the civil works for the construction of the
polyclinic at Safai; completed (August 2009) it two years after the scheduled
date of completion; and handed it over to the Department in August 2009. Due
to delayed acquisition of the land on account of dispute, UPPCL commenced

IRRIGATION DEPARTMENT

3.4.3 Undue aid to the contractor

Payment of secured advance, in violation of financial rules, led to undue
aid of रैं 1.51 crore to the contractor.

Para 456 of Financial Hand Book Volume VI provides that advances to
contractors are as a rule prohibited and every endeavour should be made to
maintain a system under which no payments are made except for work
actually done. The cases in which a contractor, whose contract is for finished
work, requires an advance on the security of materials brought to site,
divisional officers may, in such cases, make advances provided that materials
are of an imperishable”* nature. No secured advances are to be made for any
material(s), unless they are to be used within three months. Adjustments
should be made from the contractor’s bills for work done as the materials are
used.

® Revised Directorate’s letter no .1244 dated 26 June 2009.

Technician: Two; Gynecologist Assistant: Four; Surgeon: Two; and Others: 62.

* June 2009, October 2010, January 2013, April 2013 and May 2013 for staff and funds.

*‘Imperishable materials include bricks, rolled steel joists, etc. while articles such as lime, sand, kankar, etc. are
perishable,

Chapter 3 - Audit of Transactions

Scrutiny of the records (December 2012) of the Executive Engineer, Irrigation
Construction Division Matatila, Lalitpur (EE) revealed that the Government
accorded (July 2008) administrative approval and financial sanction for the
construction of Jamrar dam, an earthen dam, project for हैं 66.23 crore. The
Superintending Engineer, Irrigation Construction Circle, Jhansi executed an
agreement” for the construction of the dam from Km 1.200 to 2.650 at a cost
of % 8.38 crore with scheduled dates of commencement as 15 June 2009 and
completion by 14 June 2011. We observed that the division paid € 9.01 crore
including secured advance of €1.51 crore’ to the contractor against 12858.47
cum of sand brought to the site for laying in the filter of the dam, although the
sand is defined as a perishable item in the financial rules. The secured advance
was not recovered’®! by EE. Thus, the payment of secured advance, in
violation of financial rules, led to undue aid of = 1.51 crore to the contractor.

On this being pointed out in audit (December 2012) EE stated that the
payment was made as per requirement and recovery would be made from the
next bill. The reply was not correct as the secured advance against a perishable
material was not permissible under the financial rules.

The Government, during discussion (November 2013), while accepting the
facts and figures, stated that action would be ensured against the concerned
officers by fixing their responsibility for payment of secured advance on
perishable item in violation of financial rules.

irregularly, leading to non-procurement of other vital equipment.

The purchase of medical equipment should be synchronised with the
completion of civil works so that the equipment is installed and the patients
derive the benefit from the specialised medical facilities.

In order to provide specialized medical facilities, the Government sanctioned
(July 2010) ₹ 10.15 crore for purchase/installation of one MRI machine of

December 2013 (revised) and the MRI machine was to be installed in this
Block. Scrutiny of the records of office of the Director General, Medical &

MEDICAL EDUCATION AND TRAINING DEPARTMENT

3.4.5 Unfruitful expenditure

Expenditure of = 3.37 crore on renovation of the electric lines and power
sub-station works in Sarojini Naidu Medical College, Agra was rendered
unfruitful as the works were commenced without the requisite ‘No
Objection Certificate’ from the Electrical Safety Directorate, Lucknow.

As per the Indian Electricity Rules'™, 1956, a ‘No Objection Certificate’
(NOC) for electrical installations should be obtained from the Electrical Safety
Directorate, Lucknow prior to commencement of works.

We during scrutiny (November 2012) of the records of the office of the
Principal, Sarojini Naidu Medical College, Agra (SNMC) observed that the
Government, on the recommendations of the Twelfth Finance Commission,
sanctioned (March 2008) ₹ 2.80 crore (revised'®*) for increasing the power
load from the existing 630 KVA to 1260 KVA by renovating the electric lines
and power sub-station works'”® in the premises of SNMC and its associated
hospitals, Agra. The works were assigned (March 2008) to Processing and
Construction Cooperative Federation Limited (PACCFED), Agra for
completion by December 2010. PACCFED and the Principal, SNMC, Agra
were responsible to oversee the said work.

We further observed that PACCFED was to commence the work after the
detailed estimates, designs and drawings of the works were approved and
technically sanctioned by the Electrical Safety Directorate and NOC was
obtained in terms of the Indian Electricity Rules ibid. However, PACCFED
commenced the work without obtaining the required approval of the designs
and drawings; completed (December 2010) it at a cost of
र 2.80 crore; and handed (October 2012) it over to the Principal, SNMC,
Agra. A sum of = 56.92 lakh was also paid (March 2011) to the power supply
company’”” for increasing the power load to 1260 KVA. However, the power
supply company did not increase the power load as of August 2013, for want
of the required NOC from the Electrical Safety Directorate, Lucknow. Thus,
due to failure to obtain the required NOC, the expenditure of = 3.37 crore was
rendered unfruitful.

The Principal, SNMC, Agra stated (November 2012) that PACCFED was to
arrange the approval of the drawings and designs of the work. However, the
fact remains that the Director had allowed the renovation of the works of

REVENUE DEPARTMENT

3.4.6 Unfruitful expenditure

Multipurpose meeting hall in the premises of the Collectorate, District
Ballia, was lying incomplete even after lapse of over eight years of its
sanction as the revised estimates were not sanctioned. This led to
unfruitful expenditure of € 2.33 crore.

To facilitate the holding of meetings of the administrative officers etc., the
Government sanctioned (March 2005) construction of a multipurpose meeting
hall in the premises of the Collectorate in Ballia at हैं 2.33 crore (on the basis
of preliminary estimate). The construction works comprising civil (रे 1.76
crore) and electrical (¥ 57 lakh) were assigned (March 2005) to the Uttar
Pradesh Rajkiya Nirman Nigam (UPRNN).

Scrutiny of the records of the office of the District Magistrate, Ballia (DM)
revealed (March 2012) that UPRNN commenced (September 2005) the
construction works for the multipurpose meeting hall without technical
approval and also without fixing any time-frame for its completion, in
violation of the Government Order (March 2005) under which the construction
works were to be taken up only after technical approval was accorded by the
competent authority and fixing the time schedule for its completion. Six
months after the commencement of the works, UPRNN accorded technical
approval for only electrical works (% 57 lakh) in March 2006.

In February 2009, when the construction works was in progress, UPRNN
submitted a revised estimate of हैं 4.82 crore, duly approved by DM to the
Government, incorporating therein few additional item of works such as
inclined slab, fittings of aluminium doors and windows, boring of tube-well
etc. and also increase in the cost. However, the revised estimate was submitte
without the schedule for completion, site plan etc. Further, neither UPRNN
rectified these issues in four years of its submission of the revised estimates
nor the Government took any action for including time schedule and site plan.
The Government did not approve the revised estimate as of August 2013.
Meanwhile, UPRNN spent % 2.33 crore on the construction and the 30 per
cent works were lying incomplete as of August 2013. Meanwhile, after over
seven years of commencement of the works, UPRNN accorded technical
approval to the civil work for = 1.76 crore in March 2013.

Chapter 3 - Audit of Transactions

Thus, multipurpose meeting hall in the premises of the Collectorate, Ballia
was incomplete even after lapse of over eight years of its sanction due to non-
sanction of the revised estimate by the Government even after over four years
of the submission of the proposal, thereby rendering the expenditure of % 2.33
crore unfruitful. Besides, the objective of constructing the hall remained
unachieved.

During discussion (January 2014), the Government confirmed the facts and
figures and assured that order will be issued soon for completion of the work.

SOCIAL WELFARE DEPARTMENT AND
HOME (POLICE) DEPARTMENT
3.4.7. Non-rehabilitation of beggars

Either no or negligible number of beggars were trained and
rehabilitated during 2006-13 despite expenditure of हैं 14.96 crore on
salary, allowances, rent and electricity on maintenance of eight certified
institutions for beggars as the police did not apprehend them.

Under the Uttar Pradesh Prohibition of Beggary Act, 1975, the Government
established eight Certified Institutions (CIs) at Agra, Allahabad, Faizabad (two
Cls), Lucknow, Kanpur Nagar, Mathura and Varanasi with a capacity of 200
beggars each. The objective was to eliminate beggary in public places by
providing training to them for rehabilitation.

Scrutiny of the records (April 2012) of the office of the Director, Social
Welfare, U.P. Lucknow (Director) and subsequent information collected
revealed that the Government established eight CIs at Agra, Allahabad,
Faizabad, Kanpur Nagar and Mathura in rented buildings and those at
Lucknow and Varanasi in Government buildings during 1975 and 1976 and
also sanctioned 178 posts of various cadres for their maintenance. However,
while no beggars were detained in CIs at Kanpur Nagar, Allahabad and
Faizabad during 2006-13, the number of beggars detained, for training and
rehabilitation, was much below the capacity'” during the same period in Cls
at Agra, Lucknow, Mathura and Varanasi. The overall capacity utilisation of
these eight CIs was only one per cent during 2006-13.

Further, under the Act ibid, police is empowered to apprehend beggars and to
detain them at the aforesaid Cls for six months after obtaining orders from the
court. As per the Government Order (August 1987), police should conduct
raids at district level twice a month to apprehend beggars. Notwithstanding the
provision in the Act and the Government order, the Police either did not
conduct raids at all to apprehend the beggars or conducted very few raids
compared to the prescribed number of 24 raids in a year. Nevertheless Cls
were functional and rent of = 20.97 lakh & electricity charges of हैं 4.41 lakh

"© Lucknow: Four (2006-07), 12 (2007-08), 12 (2008-09), Nil during 2009-10 and 2010-11, one in 2011-12 and Nil
in 2012-13. Varanasi: Nil in 2006-07, five in 2007-08, 11 in 2008-09, Nil in 2009-10 to 2012-13. Agra: Nil in
2006-07, 48 in 2007-08, 37 in 2008-09, Nil in 2009-10 to 2012-13. Mathura: Nil 2006-07, 10 in 2007-08, 24 in
2008-09, 28 in 2009-10, Nil in 2010-11 & 2011-12, 41 in 2012-13.

ee


Audit Report (Gener:

was paid and deployed personnel of various cadres (ranging from 97 to 136)

were paid pay and allowances of ¢ 14.71 crore during 2006-13.

Thus, the objective to eliminate beggary in public places by providing training
to them for rehabilitation was not achieved despite the expenditure of % 14.96
crore during 2006-13 on eight CIs as the police did not apprehend them.
Further, this was indicative of lack of coordination between the two
departments of the same Government.

During discussion (September 2013), the Government while admitting the
facts, stated that an action plan will be prepared for identifying the beggars,
apprehending them and detaining them in Cls for imparting training to
rehabilitate them.

VOCATIONAL EDUCATION DEPARTMENT

3.4.8 Non-commencement of training courses and unproductive
expenditure

No training course was commenced even after six years of establishment
of State Staff Training and Research Centre in the premises of Industrial
Training Institute Lucknow, thereby rendering the expenditure of
₹ 6.39 crore on construction of the building for the Centre unproductive.

With a view to provide training to the principals of the Government / private
Industrial Training Institutes (ITI) and development of skill and training
methodology for trainers, etc., by conducting various courses'!’, the
Vocational Education Department established (January 2007) State Staff
Training and Research Centre (Centre) in the premises of ITI, Lucknow and
accordingly sanctioned (January 2007) 50 posts under various cadres. The
trainees were mainly to comprise!!! Scheduled Castes / Scheduled Tribes. The
Social Welfare Department also sanctioned (January 2007) = 6.39 crore
(revised in June 2009) for construction of a three storied building, comprising
mainly seven halls, 34 rooms and one each meeting hall, conference hall and
library, for the Centre in the premises of ITI, Lucknow. The construction
works were assigned to the Uttar Pradesh Jal Nigam for completion by March
2009.

Scrutiny of the records of office of the Principal, ITI, Lucknow revealed
(January 2012) that the training courses were to commence immediately in the
premises of ITI, Lucknow, using its class-rooms, workshop and hall, and the
available manpower. Accordingly, the Director, Training and Employment,
Lucknow, constituted (March 2007) a Committee!!”, under the chairmanship
of Additional Director, Training and Employment for commencement of the
courses. Further, the Nigam commenced (February 2007) the construction
work of the building for the Centre, completed it at ¥ 6.39 crore and handed

Strengthening of Government grainage and Government Chawki rearing centre for production
of quality seeds and supply of healthy Chawki silk-worm to the farmers

1. | Work of construction of chawki rearing house at Government Silk Farm, 0.05
Saharanpur was awarded (May 2011) to PACCFED at हैं 10 lakh and funds of
% five lakh was released to construction agency in \May 2011, which was to be
completed by August 2011. Due to non-release of funds by the department, the
rearing house was incomplete as of July 2013.

2. | हैं two lakh was released (February 2011) to Deputy Director, Sericulture, 0.02
Mainpuri for creation of irrigation facility. Of the released amount, = 1.05 lakh
and % 0.58 lakh was provided to Minor Irrigation Department and UP Power
Corporation Limited (UPPCL) for boring and electrification works respectively
in Government Silk Farm, Aspura, Mainpuri. Out of the release amount,
₹ 0.37 lakh was spent on purchase of submersible pump-set by Deputy Director,
Sericulture, Mainpuri. Subsequently, MI Department surrendered (May 2013)
% 1.05 lakh as the area was categorized under dark zone*. This resulted in
wasteful expenditure of ~ 0.58 lakh, incurred on obtaining power connection and
% 0.37 lakh incurred on purchase of submersible pump-set, could have been
avoided, had the activity been taken up after proper survey of the site.

* | Strengthening of silk koya production marketing and spinning units and establishment of silk
banks and marketing information centre

3. | % 26.35 lakh was spent on construction (2011-12) of rearing houses in 0.26
Government farms of Hatha Khurd (completed in September 2012) and Doma
(completed in October 2012) in Basti and Maharajganj respectively. Due to non-
availability of funds, required equipment could not be purchased. As a result, the
desired objective was not achieved.

4. | For construction of nine works at Silk Exchange, Varanasi, ~ 226.08 lakh was 2.26
released (March—June 2010) to Processing and Construction Cooperative
Federation Limited (PACCFED. Of nine works, four works, valuing
% 81.19 lakh, were not taken up (September 2013). Joint physical inspection also
revealed that the construction work was still incomplete.

5. | Without ensuring the title of the land, € 16.43 lakh was released (June 2009) to 0.16
PACCFED for three borings at silk farms (Tusser Farm in Leduki) in Mirzapur,
for creating irrigation facilities in the farm. The funds released to PACCFED was
utilised completely. Before awarding the work, however, the title of the land was
not ensured by the Sericulture Department. A joint survey conducted (August
2009) by Sericulture, Forest and Revenue Departments indicated that the land
was registered as forest land. As a result, the completed borings were not taken
over by the Sericulture Department, resulting in non-achievement of the
objective of providing irrigation facility to the farm.

C | Setting up of Agri-marts at Lucknow and Hapur

8. | SLSC approved (March 2008) the projects for establishment of Agrimarts in 2.63
Lucknow and Hapur with the objective to provide integrated services and
products of agriculture and allied sector under one roof. The project was
scheduled to be completed in September 2010. Despite lapse of 3 years from the
date of completion and incurring expenditure of हैं 5.46 crore, only Agrimart at
Lucknow could be established. The Agrimart at Hapur was incomplete
(September 2013) even after incurring an expenditure of € 2.63 crore.

In joint physical verification (July 2013) of Agrimart at Lucknow, it was found
that basic facilities’ for the farmers were not available.

Strengthening and renovation of communication infrastructure at HQRS

| SLSC approved project on ‘Strengthening and renovation of communication 2.45
infrastructure at HQRS” under Stream-Il, at हैं 2.45 crore. Accordingly, the
GoUP nominated (December 2009) UP Avas Evam Vikas Parishad as
construction agency for the project and a MoU was signed (December 2009) with
the agency for completion of the project by December 2010. The project,
however, was incomplete (March 2013), even after 27 months from the
scheduled date of completion of work and incurring expenditure of % 2.16 crore
due to delay in site selection procedure of OHT with Geological Survey of India
and Archaeological Survey of India.
